(SRS-RGI)

151 
 
Hospital, New -born Stabilization Units (NBSUs) are established at First Referral 
Units (FRUs)/ Community Health Centres (CHCs) for care of sick and small babies.  
 Kangaroo Mother Care (KMC) is implemented at facility and community level for low 
birth weight/ pre-term babies. It includes early and prolonged skin-to-skin contact with 
the mother or family member and exclusive and frequent breastfeeding. 
 Community Based care of  Newborn and Young Children: Under Home Based New -
born Care (HBNC) and Home -Based Care of Young Children (HBYC) program, 
home visits are performed by ASHAs to improve child -rearing practices and to 
identify sick newborn and young children in the community for referral to health 
facility. 
 Janani Shishu Suraksha Karyakram (JSSK): Sick infants up to one year of age are 
entitled to free treatment in public health institutions along with the provision of free 
transport, diagnostics, medicines, blood and consumables. 
 Social Awareness and Actions to Neutralize Pneumonia Successfully (SAANS) 
initiative has been implemented since 2019 for reduction of childhood morbidity and 
mortality due to Pneumonia.  
 STOP Diarrhoea campaign is implemented for promoting use of ORS and Zinc and 
for reducing morbidity and mortality due to childhood diarrhoea. 
 Rashtriya Bal SwasthyaKaryakram (RBSK): Children from 0 to 18 years of age are 
screened for 32 health conditions (i.e.  Diseases, Deficiencies, Defects and 
Developmental delay) un der Rashtriya Bal SwasthyaKaryakram (RBSK) to improve 
child survival. 442 District Early Intervention Centres (DEICs) at district health 
facility level are established for confirmation and management of children screened 
under RBSK. 
 Full Immunisation Coverage of 98% was achieved for all children by one year of age 
in the year ended on 31st March 2025. 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.25.3 The Committee appreciates the comprehensive response of the Department and 
acknowledges the substantial progress achieved under  the National Health Mission 
(NHM) in reducing Maternal Mortality Ratio (MMR) and Infant Mortality Rate (IMR), 
as well as expanding newborn and child health interventions across the country. The 
Committee commends that India has already achieved the Nation al Health Policy target 
for MMR and is on track to achieve the SDG target ahead of 2030. However, the 
Committee remains concerned about the persisting regional disparities, particularly in 
States where MMR and IMR remain significantly higher than the natio nal average, 
such as Assam, Bihar, and Rajasthan. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the 
Department must adopt a region-specific, evidence -based strategy  with enhanced 
financial and technical support to high -burden MMR and IMR States. The Committee 
further urges the Department to strengthen data analytics and monitoring systems 
under NHM to enable real-time tracking of maternal and infant health outcomes, and to 
ensure convergence of RMNCAH+N initiatives with social determinants such as 
nutrition, sanitation, and women’s education. The Committee also desires that specific 
Action Plan be formulated and periodic progress reports highlighting measurable 

152 
 
improvements in high -burden incidence of MMR and IMR in States be prioritized for 
review and revisit f or requisite interventions till desirable result is not achieved under 
NHM, as a whole. 
 
3.26 Functioning of Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO) 
 
Recommendations/Observations 
3.26.1 The Committee, on examination of the submissions made by the Department, 
expresses concerns that the CDSCO's current licensing process is plagued by delays, 
inconsistent timelines, and a lack of transparency, hindering the growth of the medical device 
industry. The Committee expresses serious concern that due to  rigid process and inordinate 
delays by CDSCO, a large number of manufacturing units have gone to Vietnam and 
Malaysia.  Rather, several medical devices manufacturers are scared to set up a unit in India 
due to delaying tactics of the CDSCO.  The Committee  recommends the implementation of a 
fully digitized, time -bound, and trackable licensing system. This system must include: (a) 
clearly defined timelines for each stage of the licensing process, with automatic notifications 
to applicants; (b) a standardized , publicly accessible database detailing all applications, 
queries, and approvals, allowing for real-time tracking; (c) the utilization of AI and automated 
systems to pre -screen applications for completeness, reducing the need for repeated queries 
and human intervention, especially for globally certified, non -invasive products; (d) a system 
that records all queries and the reason for the query along with the date of the query and the 
date of the reply. The Committee is of the belief that this will allow for  easy tracking of the 
process, and will allow for analysis of where the process is being slowed. This will help to 
identify bottlenecks. This will promote transparency, reduce discretionary decision -making, 
and foster a more efficient and predictable regulatory environment. 
(Para 4.4.10 of the report) 
Action Taken 
3.26.2 The Medical Devices Rules, 2017 have been comprehensively revised the medical 
device regulations with emphasis on ease of doing business while ensuring the safety and 
efficacious quality of  medical devices. CDSCO launched a portal named MedTech Mitra in 
coordination with ICMR, CDSCO and NITI AYOG in December 2023 to give handhold 
support to the innovator, Manufacturer, Start -up on regulatory clearance, development of 
clinical study plan etc.  in Medical Device Domain. More than 344 innovators have been 
provided supports so far in development and approval of the Medical Devices. There is a 
delegation of authority for issues of licenses for medical devices. Licenses for Class A and B 
medical dev ices are issued by State Licensing authority concerned and Class C and D by 
CDSCO. Various measures have been taken to streamline processing of applications as under: 
- 
(i) CDSCO has implemented MD Online which is an online portal for submission 
of the various  applications related to manufacturing and import of medical 
devices and their clinical investigation. MD Online provides paperless and 
completely faceless grant of various clearances in a time-bound manner. 
 

153 
 
(ii) In accordance with MDR 2017, phase -wise regulat ion of all medical devices 
has been implemented to ensure the safety, efficacy and quality of medical 
devices.  
 
(iii) Initially all Class A medical devices were under the licensing regime. Chapter 
III B was introduced in the Rules for registration instead of li censing of class A 
non-sterile and non -measuring medical devices vide GSR 777 (E) dated 14 
October 2022. This has eliminated the licensing requirements for a large part of 
Class A devices. A license is not needed for this category. 
 
(iv) To address such issues relating to manpower and capacity of handling medical 
device applications, a Medical Device Vertical has been created. Recruitment 
Rules have been published vide GSR 689 (E) dated 06.11.2024 for the post of 
Drugs Inspector (Medical Devices), Assistant Drug s Controller (I), Deputy 
Drugs Controller (I), Joint Drugs Controller (I), and Additional Drugs 
Controller (I). The Recruitment process has been initiated to fill such positions. 
 
(v) The Rules also provide for the registration of Medical Devices Testing 
Laboratories which are responsible for testing medical devices on behalf of 
manufacturers. Currently, 73 laboratories are registered with CDSCO for this 
purpose. Also, CDSCO has registered 14 notified bodies to perform their duties 
and functions concerning Class A and Class B medical devices. 
 
(vi) Various guidance documents, Frequently Asked Questions (FAQ), specimen 
proforma for submission of applications, and checklists for various applications 
have been published for transparency and ease of document submission b y the 
applicants. 
 
(vii) Trainings are conducted on the regulatory process and stakeholder meetings are 
held periodically. In the Financial Year 2023 -24 and 2024 -25 CDSCO has 
trained 22854 and 20551 persons respectively. Further, CDSCO is also 
deputing officers for international training program/workshop with aim to 
upgrade knowledge on the best regulatory practices being followed by stringent 
regulatory authorities across the Globe and to facilitate sharing of information 
with Indian medical devices regulation for harmonization. 
 
(viii) Dedicated public relations offices are established in CDSCO HQ and zonal 
offices with a mandate to act as an interface between CDSCO and its 
stakeholders, guide stakeholders on regulatory pathway including startup/new 
entrepreneurs, for disposal of grievances of stakeholders by addressing issues 
including at the level of Drugs Controller General of India. 
 
(ix) Keeping in view the focus of Government on ease of doing business in the 
country, Drug Controller General of India (DCGI) holds open w alk-in meetings 
with stakeholders twice a week. These meetings are held twice a week. During 

154 
 
these sessions, the DCGI addresses the problems, complaints, grievances and 
suggestions of the stakeholders, aiming for swift resolution. More than 238 
stakeholders have taken benefit of the process in last 14 months, since it started. 
 
(x) The CDSCO is also conducting interactive meetings with the industry 
association from time to time to discuss issue and challenges of the industry for 
its remedial measure. Six such i nteractive meetings were conducted by DCG(I) 
during the last two years. Further, over 180 meetings with State Drugs 
Controller and inter -ministerial meetings were conducted by CDSCO on 
various technical matters related to Medical Devices sector. 
 
3.26.3 CDSCO has also organized various awareness workshops prior to and post 
notification on Revision of Schedule M during the year 2023 -2024 and 2024 -2025 across 
India for Pharma manufacturers through offline as well as online mode. A total of 21720 and 
12522 ind ustry representatives respectively participated in the training programs. India has 
been accepted as an affiliate member of IMDRF in 2024 which is a collaborative group of 
global medical device regulators dedicated to accelerating the harmonization and convergence 
of international medical device regulations, based on a detailed study of process of the Indian 
NRA. This will give an impetus to the effort to make India a manufacturing power house for 
medical devices. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.26.4 The Committee acknowledges the various initiatives undertaken by the Ministry 
and CDSCO, such as the implementation of the MD Online portal, creation of a Medical 
Devices Vertical , establishment of testing laboratories and notified bodies , and the 
launch of the MedTech Mitra platform to support innovators and startups. While these 
measures are commendable steps towards improving regulatory efficiency and 
transparency, the Committee observes that persistent industry concerns  remain 
regarding delays, inconsis tent timelines, and lack of end -to-end real -time tracking of 
licensing applications. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Department 
must ensure the complete digitization and automation of the licensing process  through a 
unified and integrated dig ital platform linking central and state licensing authorities, 
with mandatory public disclosure of application status, query logs, and processing 
timelines. The Committee further urges that a robust performance monitoring and 
accountability framework be instituted to track adherence to defined service timelines 
and penalize undue delays. Additionally, the Committee desires that CDSCO leverage 
AI-driven analytics to identify procedural bottlenecks, reduce manual interventions, and 
improve predictability in approvals so as to foster a globally competitive and innovation-
friendly medical device ecosystem in India. 
 
Recommendations/Observations 
3.26.5 The Committee further observes that the frequent and delayed queries raised by 
CDSCO are a major obstacle for applicants, particularly startups and entrepreneurs. The 

155 
 
Committee recommends a significant overhaul of the query process. This includes: (a) 
establishing a "single query" policy, where all necessary queries are consolidated and raised at 
once, rather than in multiple rounds; (b) implementing a system of conditional approvals 
within 45 days, based on self -declaration and submission of necessary docu mentation, 
especially for products with existing international certifications (e.g., CE, US FDA); (c) 
providing detailed, standardized guidelines and FAQs to minimize ambiguity and reduce the 
need for queries; (d) creating a dedicated support team to assis t applicants with the query 
process and provide timely clarifications. The Committee believes that this approach will 
expedite the approval process, encourage innovation, and allow businesses to adapt to the 
rapidly evolving technology landscape. 
(Para 4.4.11 of the report) 
Action Taken 
3.26.6 Under the Medical Devices Rules (MDR), 2017, the application for grant of 
manufacturing license / import license is submitted by the applicant along with requisite 
technical documents as prescribed in the fourth sched ules of the rules. Each application is a 
distinct application and is processed based on completeness of the documents required to be 
submitted and queries are raised based on the information provided in the application.  If the 
documents are inadequate, query is raised to provide incomplete documents. Opportunities for 
clarification are provided to applicants. Further, to have more clarity on the specific 
requirements of technical documents to be submitted by the applicants, tooltips have been 
provided on th e portal for ease of understanding of such requirements to reduce the query 
burden. It is clarified that for applications complete in all respects, the license is issued well 
before the stipulated timeline prescribed in MDR 2017. Further, CDSCO has publish ed the 
various guidance documents, risk classification, Frequently Asked Questions (FAQ), 
specimen proforma for submission of various applications, and checklists with tooltips to 
minimize ambiguity. These documents are dynamic and being updated from time to time to 
incorporate more information for better clarity in the requirements of the medical device 
rules. Recently, updated FAQs on medical device regulations were released on 03.04.2025, 
covering key aspects like biocompatibility testing, sterilization,  licensing, and more. These 
FAQs provide crucial insights into the regulatory landscape for medical devices in India and 
reduce need for industry to visit CDSCO office for seeking clarifications. 
3.26.7 Public relations offices (PRO) are established in CDS CO HQ and zonal offices with a 
mandate to act as an interface between CDSCO and its stakeholders including start -ups/new 
entrepreneurs to guide on regulatory pathways, disposal of query process and clarification and 
also by addressing the issues including at the level of Deputy Drugs Controller (India) and 
CDSCO officers are interacting with stakeholders to have hand hold supports on their queries 
/ concerns.  Keeping in view the focus of Government on ease of doing business in the 
country, Drug Controller G eneral of India (DCGI) holds open walk -in meetings with 
stakeholders twice a week. These meetings are held twice a week. During these sessions, the 
DCGI addresses the problems, complaints, grievances and suggestions of the stakeholders, 
aiming for swift re solution. The CDSCO is also conducting interactive meetings with the 
industry association from time to time to discuss issue and challenges of the industry for its 
remedial measure. Six such interactive meetings were conducted by DCG (I) during the last 

156 
 
two years. Further, over 180 meetings with State Drugs Controller and inter -ministerial 
meetings were attended/ conducted by CDSCO on various technical matters related to 
Medical Devices sector. 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.26.8 The Committee appreciates the st eps taken by the Department and CDSCO to 
enhance transparency and reduce procedural ambiguities, including the introduction of 
tooltips on the MD Online portal , publication of updated FAQs and guidance 
documents, establishment of Public Relations Offices ( PROs), and regular stakeholder 
consultations and open meetings  by the DCGI. However, the Committee observes that 
despite these initiatives, applicants —particularly startups and small -scale 
manufacturers—continue to face multiple and sequential queries, causing delays and 
uncertainty in approval timelines. The Committee, therefore, recommends the 
Department for implementing a ―single comprehensive query‖ system  to ensure that all 
observations are raised i n one go, thereby minimizing repetitive communication and 
expediting approvals. The Committee further recommends the adoption of a time-bound 
conditional approval mechanism , especially for products possessing recognized 
international certifications such as CE or USFDA, to promote ease of doing business and 
encourage innovation. The Committee also desires CDSCO to establish a dedicated 
regulatory facilitation cell for startups within the MedTech Mitra framework to provide 
real-time query assistance and guida nce, ensuring that India’s regulatory environment 
becomes more predictable, responsive, and innovation-friendly. 
 
Recommendations/Observations 
3.26.9 The Committee acknowledges that the CDSCO faces challenges related to capacity 
and expertise, contributing  to delays and inconsistencies in the licensing process. The 
Committee opines the acceleration of the recruitment of qualified personnel, particularly 
Drugs Inspectors and other key positions in the Medical Device Vertical; and exploring the 
lateral entry of professionals from the medical device industry to bring in specialized 
expertise and a more industry -friendly perspective. The Committee urges upon the 
Department to implement a system of regular, mandatory training programs for CDSCO 
officials to enhan ce their understanding of the latest technologies, regulatory best practices, 
and their role as facilitators of industry growth; and implement a system of rotating officials 
to prevent the development of vested interests and ensure a fresh perspective. The  Committee 
believes that these measures will strengthen CDSCO's capabilities, improve efficiency, and 
foster a more collaborative relationship with the industry. 
(Para 4.4.12of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.26.10 Acknowledging the specific regulatory needs o f medical devices, a separate Medical 
Device Vertical has been created in CDSCO headquarters with officers having specific 
domain knowledge. Recruitment Rules have been published vide GSR 689 (E) dated 
06.11.2024 for the post of Drugs Inspector (Medical De vices), Assistant Drugs Controller (I), 

157 
 
Deputy Drugs Controller (I), Joint Drugs Controller (I), and Additional Drugs Controller (I). 
Recruitment process has been initiated to fill up the newly created posts.  Various training 
programs are being conducted w ith the State and Central Drug Controller officers to have 
uniformity in implementation of Medical Devices Rules throughout the country. In the 
Financial Year 2023 -24 and 2024 -25 CDSCO has trained 22854 and 20551 persons 
respectively. Further, CDSCO is als o deputing officers for international training 
program/workshop with aim to upgrade knowledge on the best regulatory practices being 
followed by stringent regulatory authorities across the Globe and to facilitate sharing of 
information with Indian medical devices regulation for harmonization.  CDSCO also has a 
transfer policy in place for its officers for encouraging diverse exposure. 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.26.11 The Committee notes with appreciation the establishment of a dedicated 
Medical Device Vertical within CDSCO and the initiation of the recruitment process  
under the newly notified Recruitment Rules (GSR 689 E, dated 06.11.2024) , as well as 
the extensive training and international exposure programs conducted to enhance 
regulatory capacity. Ho wever, the Committee emphasizes that the recruitment process 
for key technical and regulatory positions must be expedited to ensure the vertical 
operates at full strength and delivers consistent, timely outcomes. The Committee 
further recommends that the lateral induction of domain experts  from the medical 
device industry, academia, and research institutions be actively pursued to bridge the 
skill gap and introduce global best practices. Additionally, CDSCO may consider 
establishing a structured, continuous professional development framework with periodic 
certification and assessment modules to ensure sustained competency and regulatory 
excellence. The Committee understands the pivotal role of a robust and transparent 
rotation policy to prevent complacency a nd ensuring that officers gain diverse field and 
headquarters experience, thereby strengthening regulatory integrity and fostering a 
more proactive, collaborative engagement with the medical device industry. 
 
 
Recommendations/Observations 
3.26.12 The Committee would like to express that the perception of CDSCO as a 
"license raj" highlights a critical need for improved communication and collaboration with the 
medical device industry. The Committee, therefore, recommends the establishment of an 
independent Industry Advisory Board, comprising representatives from manufacturers 
(including startups), importers, healthcare providers, and experts. This board would provide 
regular feedback to CDSCO on regulatory challenges and potential improvements; partic ipate 
in the development of new guidelines and policies; and serve as a platform for resolving 
disputes and addressing industry concerns. Furthermore, CDSCO should implement a system 
of regular stakeholder consultations, including open forums, workshops, and online platforms, 
to gather feedback and address concerns proactively. This will foster a more transparent and 
collaborative regulatory environment, build trust between CDSCO and the industry, and 
ensure that regulations are aligned with the needs of a rapidly evolving sector, moving 

158 
 
CDSCO from a perceived roadblock to a true facilitator. This will also create a clear channel 
for industry concerns to be heard and addressed, and allow for a more dynamic and 
responsive regulatory approach. 
(Para 4.4.13 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.26.13 Public relations offices (PRO) are established in CDSCO HQ and zonal offices 
with a mandate to act as an interface between CDSCO and its stakeholders, guide 
stakeholders on regulatory pathway including start -ups/new entrepreneurs, for disposal of 
grievances of stakeholders by addressing issues including at the level of Drugs Controller 
General of India. A platform has been developed in CDSCO headquarter for direct interaction 
with Drugs Controller India and the applic ant/innovators/start-up, twice a week without any 
appointment in Walk -in set up, to address their concern and give guidance on various 
regulatory issues. More than 238 stakeholders have taken benefit of the process in last 14 
months, since it started. As a  stakeholder outreach, the CDSCO started workshops, webinar, 
etc. so as to have direct interaction with the stakeholders and to give clarity on regulatory 
requirements, fill up the gap for implementation of new provisions, address the challenges 
being face d by the stakeholder from time to time etc.  Recently, two such programs were 
organised and further two more such program is scheduled in the month of May and July, 
2025, respectively. These programs will be continued in future. Further, the CDSCO will 
continue to interact with the industry stakeholder on a regular basis to gather feedback and 
address their concerns for smooth functioning of the regulatory system. 
3.26.14 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare has constituted Medical Devices 
Technical Adviso ry Group (MDTAG) to advise CDSCO on matter related to regulation of 
medical devices consisting of subject experts and representatives from industry associations. 
The latest meeting of MDTAG has been held on 28.04.2025.  A MedTech Mitra portal also 
established in coordination with ICMR, CDSCO and NITI AYOG December 2023 to give 
handhold support to the innovator, Manufacturer, Start -up on regulatory clearance, 
development of clinical study plan etc. in Medical Device Domain. More than 344 innovators 
have been provided supports so far in development and approval of the Medical Devices. The 
Drugs Technical Advisory Board (DTAB) under the Drugs & Cosmetics Act, 1940 is a 
statuary Board, to advise the Government on technical matters to frame new policy etc. The 
Board has two members from the industry associations in its constitution and the nominated 
representative of industry associations are also called for deliberation of the technical matters 
for framing the new policy, etc. during its meetings.   
3.26.15 The CD SCO is also conducting interactive meetings with the industry 
association from time to time to discuss issue and challenges of the industry for its remedial 
measure. Six such interactive meetings were conducted by DCG(I) during the last two years. 
Further, over 180 meetings with State Drugs Controller and inter -ministerial meetings were 
attended/ conducted by CDSCO on various technical matters related to Medical Devices 
sector. CDSCO has started Industry Awareness Programs for regulatory guidance and 
addressing the concerns of industry while implementation of requirements of the MDR, 2017.  

159 
 
Recently, two such programmes was organised and further two more such program is 
scheduled in the month of May and July, 2025, respectively.  CDSCO has also organized 
various awareness workshops prior to and post notification on Revision of Schedule M during 
the year 2023-2024 and 2024-2025 across India for Pharma manufacturers through offline as 
well as online mode. A total of 21720 and 12522 industry representatives respe ctively 
participated in the training programs. Interactive meetings with the industry associations are 
held by the senior officials of the MoHFW and CDSCO.  Further, CDSCO consistently 
engages with the pharmaceutical industry across various platforms to exc hange valuable 
information and provide guidance on regulatory frameworks. This collaboration ensures that 
the medicines manufactured in the country meet the highest standards of quality, safety, and 
efficacy, safeguarding public health. 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.26.16 The Committee appreciates the initiatives undertaken by the Department 
of Health and Family Welfare and CDSCO to strengthen stakeholder engagement 
through the establishment of Public Relations Offices (PROs) , the MedTech Mitra 
portal, regular walk-in consultations, industry awareness programs, and the creation of 
the Medical Devices Technical Advisory Group (MDTAG) . These steps indicate a 
positive movement towards greater transparency and industry collaboration. The 
Committee is of the view tha t these mechanisms, while commendable, largely serve as 
consultative or facilitative platforms and do not fully meet the intent of the Committee’s 
earlier recommendation for a dedicated and independent Industry Advisory Board . The 
Committee, therefore, rec ommends that the Ministry institutionalize such a board with 
statutory or formal advisory status , comprising balanced representation from startups, 
MSMEs, major manufacturers, healthcare providers, and independent experts , to 
provide structured and periodic feedback on regulatory practices, policy evolution, and 
dispute resolution. The Committee further recommends that the outcomes of 
consultations, including minutes of stakeholder meetings and action taken reports , be 
made publicly accessible to enhance transparency and accountability. A fixed biannual 
schedule for stakeholder consultations  should also be adopted to ensure regular and 
meaningful dialogue, thereby fostering a culture of trust, responsiveness, and shared 
responsibility between the regulator and the medical device industry. 
 
3.27 Tertiary Care Programs (TCPs) 
Recommendations/Observations 
3.27.1 In the FY 2023 -24, the BE amounting to Rs. 289.62 crore was slightly reduced to Rs. 
276.39 crore at RE stage, however , the actual expenditure remained Rs. 258.83 crore. The 
Committee observes with concern over low trend of underutilization of funds, even during the 
FY 2024-25, out of the allocation of Rs. 322.90 crore at RE stage, only Rs. 170.08 crore till 
31.1.2025 hav e been utilised which translates into just 52.67% utilisation of fund. The 
Committee wishes to highlight the fund utilization within the National Mental Health 
Programme (12.46% of RE 2024-25 till 31.01.2025), National Programme for Prevention and 
Management of Trauma & Burn Injury (35.05% of RE 2024 -25 till 31.01.2025), National 

160 
 
Programme for prevention & Control of Non -Communicable Diseases (45.60% of RE 2024 -
25 till 31.01.2025) and Health Care for the Elderly (1.61% of RE 2024 -25 till 31.01.2025) 
where the percentage utilization is below 50%. The Committee also acknowledges that the 
National Tobacco Control Programme & National Drugs De -addiction Programme have 
achieved 100% of the RE which shows that this program is working well. 
(Para 4.5.4 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.27.2 The Department acknowledges the observations made by the PSC. The utilization of 
funds under the different schemes is as follows: - 
National Programme for Prevention and Management of Trauma and Burns: 
*     For the NPPMT&BI, Rs. 134 crore has been allocated for 5 years (2021 -22 to 2025-26) 
and Rs. 96.84 crore has been spent till 2024-25 (67.72%). 
*     For the year 2023-24, Rs. 11 crore was spent. 
*     For the FY 2024 -25, Rs. 18 crore was allocated and Rs. 17.73 crore was spent w hich 
comes out to be 98.5%. 
National Programme of HealthCare for Elderly: In NPHCE Programme percentage wise 
utilization of funds allocated is below 50% (1.61% of RE 2024 -25 till 3.01.2025) because 
clarification/approval was sought from the Department of E xpenditure (DoE) for extension of 
timeline for providing HR Support to all the RGC‘s under NPHCE Programme was awaited at 
that time. Thereafter, an amount of Rs. 53.02 crores were utilised which comes out to a total 
expenditure of 86.91% of RE (61 crores). 
National Programme for prevention & Control of Non -Communicable Diseases (NP -NCD): 
For the FY 2024-25, Rs 8000 Crore was allocated at RE Stage and Rs 79.38 Crore was spent 
which comes out to be 99.23%. 
National Mental Health Programme: The utilization of funds under the NMHP as on 
31.03.2025 stands at Rs. 5.27 crore against the Revised Estimates (RE) of Rs. 19.99 crore for 
FY 2024 -25, reflecting an expenditure of Rs. 26.36%.  The lower utilization of funds is 
primarily due to several factors at the State le vel due to non -fulfilment of SNA condition of 
MoF. To address these issues and support programme implementation, DO letters are sent to 
States/UTs for addressing the above issues. Further, the Central Mental Health Programme 
team visits CoEs to conduct on -site assessment of programme activities. To monitor the 
progress of implementation of programme, quarterly progress reports are collected seeking 
financial as well as physical progress report, followed by data analysis by the Central Mental 
Health team. Vi rtual review meetings with States and CoEs are also conducted at regular 
intervals. 
 
 

161 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.27.3 The Committee takes note of the Department of Health and Family Welfare’s 
detailed clarification and the subsequent improvement in fund uti lization under several 
national programmes, particularly the National Programme for Prevention and 
Management of Trauma and Burns , the National Programme for Prevention and 
Control of Non -Communicable Diseases (NP -NCD), and the National Programme for 
Health Care of the Elderly (NPHCE) , where utilization has significantly increased 
following administrative approvals. The Committee, nevertheless, is worried over the 
persistently low utilization under the National Mental Health Programme (NMHP) , 
which reflects  systemic bottlenecks such as delays in meeting financial compliance 
conditions, weak state -level implementation capacity, and inadequate coordination 
mechanisms. The Committee, therefore, recommends the Department to institute a 
robust monitoring and acco untability framework with quarterly financial and physical 
progress tracking to ensure that fund release is linked to performance indicators  rather 
than mere administrative compliance. Further, dedicated capacity -building and 
technical support units should  be strengthened further at the state level to assist with 
financial management and reporting. The Committee also recommends that the 
Department of Expenditure and Department of Health and Family Welfare must  
streamline procedural requirements for fund rel ease under centrally sponsored 
schemes, particularly for mental health interventions, so that programme delivery is not 
hampered by administrative delays. A mid-year utilization review  mechanism may also 
be instituted to identify underperforming components  and allow timely reallocation of 
unspent funds to more efficient programmes. 
 
 
3.28 Human Resource for Health and Medical Education 
Recommendations/Observations 
3.28.1 The Committee observes with concern that in the FY 2023 -24, the BE allocation 
amounting to Rs. 6500 crore was significantly reduced to Rs. 1519.87 crore at RE stage 
reflecting into huge decline by 76.62%. The Committee wishes to express further concern that 
even this reduced amount at RE stage could not be utilized fully (Actual expenditure 2023-24 
is Rs. 1321.78 crore). Adding to the woes, in the FY 2024-25 also, the budgetary allocation of 
Rs. 1274.79 crore at BE stage was decreased to Rs. 579.12 crore at RE stage.  The reduction 
in budgetary allocation under the Scheme become more alarming  and attention seeking of 
budget planner particularly when the Scheme contains components of establishment of new 
medical colleges attached with district/referral hospitals, up -gradation of State Government 
Medical Colleges for increasing the MBBS seats an d PG seats.  On the one hand, the 
Government aims at increasing patient -doctor ratio as per WHO norm for ensuring better 
health care and on the other hand, Government is significantly reducing the budgetary 
allocation.  The Government must adopt the prudent budgetary process. 
 
(Para 4.6.2 of the report) 

162 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.28.2 The recommendations of the Committee have been noted. At present, the committed 
liabilities under the schemes amount to Rs. 6,312.37 crores. The funds under the scheme are 
sanctioned based on the demands raised by the States/UTs and submission of Utilization 
Certificates in a given financial year. However, many States/UTs have struggled to raise 
demand due to non-release of matching state share. Some of the other possible reasons for t he 
same are litigations, land issues and other clearances issues. 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.28.3 The Committee takes note of the explanation submitted by the Department of 
Health and Family Welfare regarding the underutilization of funds and the reduction in 
budgetary allocation under the Centrally Sponsored Scheme for the establishment of 
new medical colleges and upgradation of existing government medical colleges. While 
the Committee acknowledges that factors such as delays in the release of matching Stat e 
share, land -related issues, and pending clearances have contributed to slower 
implementation, the Committee is deeply concerned that these recurring administrative 
and procedural bottlenecks continue to impede the expansion of critical medical 
education infrastructure. The Committee, therefore, recommends the Department to 
establish a dedicated monitoring and coordination cell  to track project -wise progress, 
expedite resolution of State -level bottlenecks, and ensure timely submission of 
Utilization Certif icates. The Committee further urges the Department to engage 
proactively with State Governments through periodic high -level review meetings  to 
address issues related to land acquisition, statutory clearances, and financial 
compliance. The Committee also em phasizes the need for advance planning and phased 
fund release mechanisms  to prevent large -scale budget cuts at the RE stage and ensure 
steady progress in achieving the national goal of improving the doctor –population ratio 
in line with WHO norms. 
 
 
Recommendations/Observations 
3.28.4 The Committee recommends that the Department of Health and Family Welfare 
implement a proactive, multi-pronged strategy to ensure consistent and efficient utilization of 
allocated funds for the Human Resource for Health & Medi cal Education schemes by 
enhancing the absorption capacity. Specifically, given the recurring issues of underutilization 
due to lack of state demand, delayed state share release, and Real -time System of Integrated 
Quick Transfers (SNA SPARSH) compliance de lays, the Department should establish a 
dedicated task force to provide intensive, institutionalised support to each state and union 
territory. This task force will conduct pre -emptive assessments of state needs, offer hands -on 
assistance with SNA SPARSH c ompliance, and facilitate timely release of state matching 
funds through regular communication and problem -solving workshops. Furthermore, the 
Department should implement a quarterly review system that incorporates direct feedback 
from states, allowing for  real-time adjustments to funding allocations and program 

163 
 
implementation. This approach will not only address the immediate issue of underutilization 
but also build stronger collaborative relationships with states, ensuring the long -term success 
of expandi ng medical education infrastructure and increasing the availability of medical 
professionals. 
(Para 4.6.6 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.28.5 The recommendations of the committee have been noted. The Government is closely 
monitoring the utilization of fund s and implementation of the SNA SPARSH guidelines by 
the States/UTs through regular review meetings at various levels of the government.  At 
present, out of 21 States/UTs notified to be implemented on SNA SPARSH model, 14 States 
have been successfully on-boarded. Further, assistance and hand -holding of the States/UTs in 
implementation of the SNA SPARSH model for release of funds under the scheme is being 
provided. 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.28.6 The Committee notes the submission of the Department in regard to 
implementation of the SNA SPARSH under Human Resource for Health and Medical 
Education system and the progress made in onboarding 14 out of 21 States/UTs under 
the SNA SPARSH model and the ongoing efforts to provide assistance to remaining 
States. The Committee, however, expresses deep concern about the continued delays and 
uneven implementation of fund utilization mechanisms, which hinder the optimal 
execution of Human Resource for Health & Medical Education schemes. The 
Committee, therefore, reiterates  its earlier recommendation that a dedicated task force 
or coordination cell be formally constituted within the Department to provide targeted, 
state-specific technical and administrative support  for SNA SPARSH compliance and 
timely fund release. This cell should maintain a real-time digital dashboard  to monitor 
the status of each State/UT, identify bottlenecks early, and escalate unresolved issues to 
higher administrative levels for prompt resolu tion. The Committee also recommends 
that capacity-building workshops  be institutionalized for State officials to strengthen 
their financial and procedural readiness. A system of quarterly performance grading of 
States based on fund utilization and compliance efficiency may further incentivize better 
performance and ensure sustained improvement in the absorption capacity and timely 
implementation of medical education and health workforce initiatives. 
 
3.29 Pradhan Mantri Ayushman Bharat Health Infrastructure Mission (PM-ABHIM) 
 
Recommendations/Observations 
3.29.1 The Committee expresses concern over the declining trend of fund utilisation under 
PM-ABHIM during the last two financial years 2023-24 and 2024-25 i.e. 42.99% and 62.74% 
respectively, of the BE allocation that casts aspersion on absorption capacity of Sch eme and 

164 
 
the professional proficiency of implementing agency of the Scheme. The Committee, 
therefore, strongly recommends a more streamlined and front -loaded disbursement process 
for the PM -ABHIM scheme as the scheme contain visionary aim and objective of 
Government to fill critical gaps in health infrastructure, surveillance and health research – 
spanning both the urban and rural areas so that the communities are Atmanirbhar in managing 
such pandemic/ health crises. The data clearly shows consistent underut ilization of allocated 
funds, particularly in the initial phases of each fiscal year, despite the capital intensive 
infrastructure project under the scheme. While the Department attributes this to the lead time 
of capital expenditure projects, the signific ant reductions from Budget Estimates to Revised 
Estimates indicate a predictable pattern of slow pace of expenditure. To address this, the 
Committee suggests that the Department implement a system where a larger portion of the 
annual budget is released dur ing the first quarter, contingent on states submitting detailed 
project implementation plans and timelines. This would allow for early initiation of 
infrastructure projects, reducing the lag time and ensuring more consistent expenditure 
throughout the fisc al year. Moreover, the Department should focus on providing technical 
assistance and capacity building to states to expedite project approvals and implementation, 
thereby mitigating delays and ensuring the timely achievement of the scheme's objectives. 
(Para 4.7.4 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.29.2 PM-ABHIM, an infrastructure initiative aimed at strengthening healthcare 
infrastructure down to the block level, operates as a capital grant. Consequently, the initial 
expenditure tends to be gradual due to the inherent lead time in infrastructure development. 
However, as the scheme approaches its final year, expenditure is anticipated to accelerate with 
the completion of ongoing projects.  To ensure appropriate expenditure progression, 
States/UTs were requested t o submit Quarterly Expenditure Plans (QEP), as outlined in the 
Ministry's D.O. letter dated April 7, 2025. Furthermore, in accordance with the Department of 
Expenditure (DoE) guidelines issued on July 13, 2023, August 4, 2023, October 4, 2024, and 
December 17, 2024, 31 States have been notified under the SNA SPARSH Platform for PM -
ABHIM, with 28 States already on boarded. This platform operates by providing States with 
expenditure limits rather than direct cash releases, enabling them to incur expenses by 
submitting daily payment files to the Ministry through their respective State Treasuries. In the 
new mechanism SNA SPARSH, up to 50% of the Allocated Budget can be given as Mother 
Sanction to the States, on request of the States, which the States can utiliz e till exhaustion of 
75% of the limit given and can apply for Mother Sanction for the remaining Budget.  
3.29.3 The Ministry of Health and Family Welfare (MoHFW) actively monitors and 
evaluates the progress of all States/UTs through regular review meetings , video conferences, 
and field visits by senior officials. Additionally, regional and state -level workshops have been 
conducted to bolster the capacities of State Program Management Units (PMUs). These 
PMUs have been established within the States to offer essential technical and administrative 
support, thereby facilitating timely construction. Regional workshops were undertaken for 
providing technical assistance & capacity building. Moreover, Common Review Missions 

165 
 
(CRMs) are conducted periodically to asses s and monitor the scheme's progress and 
implementation status.
